2026.11.11 
王章妮 C00251@ibfs.com.tw 
1476.TT 儒鴻 
 評價低緣、高殖利率，投資價值浮現

[法說會更新]   
   公司簡介 
儒鴻成立於 1977 年，生產各式針織布及成衣，從染整、織布、定型、成衣，擁有垂
直整合生產供應鏈，並切入機能性服飾市場，2025 年針織布營收 127.7 億元，YoY-
2.3%，營收占比 33.61%，成衣營收 252.2 億元，YoY+6.2%，比重占 66.39%。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
買進 416 
【前次投資建議】買進，396 
【大盤指數/股價】42712 / 348.5

➢ 3Q26 獲利略低於投研部預估 
公司 3Q26 營收 105.72 億元，YoY+9.7%，營收略低於投研部預估之
106.8 億元，主要係因成衣營收低於投研部預期所致，毛利率 31.18%，
優於投研部預期，主要受惠高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比提升，且匯率
亦有所助益(約影響毛利率 0.62 個百分點)，費用率因業績獎金提列較高
而推升至 10.49%，營益率 20.69%，低於投研部預估之 21.24%，稅後
EPS 6.36 元，低於投研部預估之 6.57 元。 
➢ 4Q26 展望正向，略為上修 4Q26 獲利預估 
10/2026 營收 34 億元，YoY+10.7%，符合投研部預期，本次法說會管
理層表示對 4Q26 營運維持 TITAS 2026 釋出之看法不變，4Q26 營收可
望為年內高峰，上修 4Q26 營收預估至 105.99 億元，YoY+14.4%，毛
利率因原料成本負面影響趨緩，公司高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比持續
提升，毛利率同步上修至 31.05%，YoY+0.86ppt.，預估稅後淨利 17.25
億元，YoY+9%，稅後 EPS 上修至 6.29 元。目前訂單能見度維持 6 個
月，展望正向，期中選舉民主黨勝選預期對關稅壁壘政策可望有所趨緩，
且美伊戰爭亦可望有所緩解，市場不確定因素減少亦有利於整體經濟消
費動能，印尼廠一期、二期目前維持穩定獲利且產能滿載，投研部上修
2026 年營收預估至 408.77 億元，YoY+7.6%，毛利率因新產品、新客
戶營收占比持續提升，略為上修至 31.09%，YoY+2.45ppt.，稅後 EPS
上修至 24.99 元。  
➢ 2027 年營運成長可期，評價處歷史區間低緣且殖利率逾 5%  
公司目前始承接 04/2027 訂單，觀察美國總體經濟動能強勁，消費者支
出可望維持韌性，惟考量公司現有廠區產能趨於滿載，目前僅以原有產
能小規模擴充為主，投研部以過去幾年公司中性成長均值預估 2027 年
營收 431.95 億元，YoY+5.7%，毛利率因 4Q27 印尼新廠投產而略為承
壓，預估 2027 年毛利率 30.62%，YoY-0.47ppt.，營益率預估 20.74%，
YoY+0.01ppt.，稅後 EPS 預估由 26.19 元略為上修至 26.31 元，投研部
以現金股利配發率 74.63%預估，現金股利殖利率可望達 5.35%，且依
未來四季合計稅後 EPS 估算目前評價仍處過往歷史區間低緣位置，長期
投資價值浮現，投研部維持評價 PER 16X 並以未來四季稅後 EPS 估算目
標價，目標價由 396 元上修至 416 元(4Q26~3Q27F PE 16X)，維持買
進投資建議。

個股報告

儒鴻為國內成衣廠龍頭 
儒鴻成立於 1977 年，生產各式針織布及成衣，從染整、織布、定型、成衣，擁有垂
直整合生產供應鏈，並切入機能性服飾市場，2025 年針織布營收 127.7 億元，YoY-
2.3%，營收占比 33.61%，成衣營收 252.2 億元，YoY+6.2%，比重占 66.39%；營收
區域別來看，2025 年美洲營收占比 50.87%，YoY+4.5%，亞洲營收占比 35.19%，
YoY+2.6%，歐洲營收占比 10.29%，YoY+10.2%，中東營收占比 2.49%，YoY-17.5%，
紐澳營收占比 0.85%。YoY+16.2%，非洲營收占比 0.31%。YoY-64.7%。 
3Q26 獲利略低於投研部預估  
公司 3Q26 營收 105.72 億元，QoQ+4.8%，YoY+9.7%，營收略低於投研部預估之
106.8 億元，主要係因成衣營收低於投研部預期所致，針織布營收則優於投研部預期，
毛利率 31.18%，QoQ+1.17ppt.，YoY+2.52ppt.，優於投研部預估之 30.76%，主要
受惠高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比提升，且匯率亦有所助益(約影響毛利率 0.62 個百
分點)，費用率因業績獎金提列較高而推升至 10.49%，高於投研部預估之 9.53%，營
益率 20.69%，QoQ+1.51ppt.，YoY+1.59ppt.，低於投研部預估之 21.24%，業外則
有匯兌收入認列 1,327 萬元，稅後淨利 17.44 億元，QoQ+12.3%，YoY+5.6%，稅
後 EPS 6.36 元，低於投研部預估之 6.57 元。

個股報告 
4Q26 展望正向，略為上修 4Q26 獲利預估 
10/2026 營收 34 億元，MoM-3.1%，YoY+10.7%，YTD+5.9%，占投研部原預估
4Q26 營收達成率 33.4%，符合投研部預期，公司過去 10 月單月營收占 4Q26 營收
占比區間介於 29.5%~34.2%，本次法說會管理層表示對 4Q26 營運維持台北紡織展
(TITAS 2026)釋出之看法不變，以訂單來看，若出貨船期順利，則 4Q26 營收可望為
年內高 峰， 基此 ，投研 部 上修 4Q26 營收 預估 至 105.99 億 元，QoQ+0.3%，
YoY+14.4%，毛利率因原料成本負面影響趨緩，公司高毛利率新產品及新客戶占比持
續提升，毛利率亦同步上修至 31.05%，QoQ-0.14ppt.，YoY+0.86ppt.，並同步上修
營益率至 20.33%，QoQ-0.36ppt.，YoY+1.17ppt.，預估稅後淨利 17.25 億元，QoQ-
1.1%，YoY+9%，稅後 EPS 由 5.84 元略為上修至 6.29 元。 
展望 2026 年，訂單能見度維持 2 個季度，訂單展望維持正向，公司表示目前訂單尚
未因美國期中選舉結果出爐後而有所變動，然期中選舉民主黨勝選預期對關稅壁壘政
策可望有所趨緩，且美伊戰爭亦可望有所緩解，市場不確定因素減少亦有利於整體經
濟消費動能，印尼廠一期、二期目前維持穩定獲利且產能滿載，目前協力廠外發比重
維持 27%~28%，因 4Q26 營收及獲利略為上修，投研部亦同步上修 2026 年營收預
估至 408.77 億元，YoY+7.6%，毛利率部份，因高毛利率新產品、新客戶營收占比可
望 持 續 提 升 ， 且 匯 率 環 境 相 較 於 2025 年 亦 較 為 友 善 ， 略 為 上 修 31.09% ，
YoY+2.45ppt.，營益率則由 20.62%上修至 20.73%，YoY+2.06ppt.，並預估稅後淨
利 68.56 億元，YoY+24.3%，稅後 EPS 由 24.77 元上修至 24.99 元。 
圖 1：公司歷年月營收趨勢               單位：千元

資料來源：國票投顧彙製 
2027 年營運可望維持成長，目前評價處歷史區間低緣且殖利率逾 5% 
展望 2027 年，目前已開始承接 04/2027 訂單，雖訂單能見度未達 2027 年底，且客
戶端 2027 年預示單仍洽談中，然觀察美國就業環境仍穩健，初領及續領失業救濟金
人數仍處歷史低檔，總體經濟動能強勁，消費者支出可望維持韌性，且 2026 年營運
受戰爭、通膨影響較為劇烈，研判 2027 年可望較為淡化，惟考量公司現有廠區產能
趨於滿載，印尼三期實際貢獻營收時點落於 4Q27~1Q28，目前僅以原有產能小規模
擴充產能為主，若市況優於預期則以外發協力廠因應，2027 年外發比重大致上介於
27%~35%區間，基此，投研部以過去幾年公司中性成長均值預估 2027 年營收 431.95
億元，YoY+5.7%，主要為產品報價提升之貢獻，並預估毛利率因 4Q27 印尼新廠投

## Document 034: 2026-11-11_［みずほ経済インサイト］堅調なAI需要で大幅増益が視野_1ea94428

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-11_［みずほ経済インサイト］堅調なAI需要で大幅増益が視野_1ea94428.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

堅調なAI 需要で大幅増益が視野 
投資増につながるかが焦点に

○ 製造業の業績見通しは大幅に改善している。輸出向けを中心とするAI関連財の需要拡大が背景。
2026年度は好業績が期待され、二桁増益が視野に入る 
○ AI関連財の増産を背景に、国内製造業の設備不足感は2025年後半以降強まっている。足元では関
連業種の機械受注が増加しており、AI需要拡大が国内設備投資に波及する兆しがみられる 
○ 設備投資は緩やかな拡大が息長く続く見通し。ただし、米国でデータセンター建設規制が実施さ
れれば、輸出・業績・設備投資の好循環が逆回転に転じるリスクも

１．企業景況感は AI 関連の製造業を中心に改善 
AIの学習・利用に必要なデータセンター投資や半導体需要が世界的に堅調であることを背景に、日
本の製造業の景況感は幅広く改善している。日銀短観（2026年9月調査）では、大企業・製造業の業況
判断DIが6月調査から改善した（図表1）。業種別では、半導体や電子部品などを含む電気機械や、半
導体製造装置を含む生産用機械の改善が際立つ。そのほか、窯業・土石製品、非鉄金属、半導体材料・
素材を扱う化学などでも改善がみられた。ロイター短観（10月調査）でも、「半導体関連市場の需要
が強い」との声が散見され、AI関連需要の堅調さがうかがえる。 
AI関連財の輸出が拡大し、足元で企業の業績見通しは大きく改善している。企業業績の改善を国内
の需要拡大につなげるうえで、次の焦点となるのが設備投資である。企業業績の改善に比べて、投資
図表 1 業況判断 DI 図表 2 日本の AI 関連財輸出額

### Page 2

2

などの内需への恩恵はまだ明確ではなく、今後設備投資の増加として発現する可能性が期待される。 
２．AI 関連財輸出は好調。2026 年度の製造業は二桁増益が視野に 
世界的なAIサービスの普及に伴い、データセンターや関連機器への投資が拡大している。大規模な
データセンターを保有・運営し、クラウドサービスやAI向けの計算基盤を提供するIT企業、とりわけ
ハイパースケーラーと呼ばれる大規模事業者を中心に、高水準の設備投資を継続する見通しが示され
ていることを踏まえると、AI関連投資の高い伸びは当面持続する見込みである。こうしたなか、モデ
ルの演算を担う処理装置のほか、大量のデータを蓄える記憶装置、サーバー同士をつなぐ通信ケーブ
ル、電力を安定供給する電源装置などといった財において投資需要が高まっている。日本企業が供給
する財についても、電子部品・機器への需要が増加しているほか、AI向け半導体の増産投資は日本の
半導体製造・検査装置や材料への需要を押し上げている。 
図表2は、輸出全体から、WTOの定義に従ってAI関連財を抽出したものだ。AI関連財輸出は2025年終
盤以降加速しており、8月時点では前年比＋29.1％と高い伸びになった。主因は半導体・電子部品で、
旺盛な需要のもとで半導体価格が大幅に上昇し、輸出金額を押し上げている。また、半導体製造・検
査機器や、データセンターに組み込まれる機械・部材、半導体の製造に用いられる原材料・素材など、
幅広いAI関連財が輸出増に寄与している。 
輸出額の高い伸びを受け、製造業では業績見通しが改善している。日銀短観（9月調査）の2026年度
経常利益計画（全規模・製造業）は、6月調査時点から大幅に上方修正され、前年度比＋11.6％の増益
が見込まれる（図表3）。特に電気機械では、上期の堅調な輸出実績を反映して、経常利益計画が大き
く上方修正された。このほか、業況判断DIの改善が目立った業種でも同様の傾向がみられた。堅調な
AI関連需要が下期も続けば、製造業では大幅増益が期待される。

### Page 3

３．AI 関連業種の投資需要は増勢加速の兆しも 
今後の実質GDPへの影響を見通すうえでの焦点は、企業収益の拡大が設備投資を中心とした内需の押
し上げにどの程度繋がるかだ。この点、AI需要の追い風を受け好業績が見込まれることを踏まえれば、
設備投資の伸びはやや物足りなさが否めない。日銀短観（9月調査）によると、製造業の2026年度設備
投資計画は直近5年間では最も低い伸びに留まっている（図表4）。建材や生産設備の価格上昇、金利
上昇に伴う資金調達コストの増加が投資負担となり、好業績のもとでも計画の積み増しを抑えている
可能性がある。 
もっとも、設備投資需要は計画対比で伸び余地がある。AI関連財を中心に増産が進むなか、能力増
強投資の必要性が高まっているためだ。AI関連財の生産は2025年秋以降、はっきりとした増加傾向が
続いている（図表5）。生産・営業用設備判断DI（全規模・製造業）も2025年後半から低下しており、
製造業の設備不足感は強まっている（図表6）。 
実際、AI需要の拡大が今後の設備投資を押し上げる兆しもみられる。図表7は、設備投資の先行指標
とされる機械受注（国内需要向け）を需要者別に分解し、価格変動の影響を取り除いたものだ。これ
をみると2025年夏場以降、情報通信機械、電気機械、はん用・生産用機械といった業種からの受注が
急増していることがわかる。結果として、半導体製造装置をはじめとするAI・データセンター関連品
目では受注残高が積み上がっている（図表8）。 
ただし、機械受注が設備投資として顕在化するまでにはタイムラグを伴うとみられる。そもそも、
受注残高が積み上がっている背景には、旺盛な需要だけでなく、供給面の制約による影響があると考
えられる1。このため、設備投資の増加ペースは緩やかなものにとどまる公算が大きいとみているが、
その分、今後は息の長い投資拡大局面が続くだろう。

図表 5 鉱工業生産 図表6 生産・営業用設備判断DI

（注） AI関連はWTOによるAI-enabling productsの定義に準ずる

### Page 4

4

４．今後は政府の投資支援策も民間投資を後押し。米国の規制強化リスクには要注意 
以上のように、先行きの設備投資は、設備不足感の強まるAI関連産業の投資需要に支えられ、緩や
かに増勢を強めると見込まれる。 
また今後は、政府の政策支援が、投資コストの負担軽減を通じて、設備投資を後押しするとみられ
る。7月末に始動した政府の「大胆な投資促進税制」では、一定の要件を満たす国内投資について、即
時償却や税額控除の適用を認める減税措置が設けられた。さらに、2027年度予算の編成では、特別予
算枠として『「強く豊かな日本」投資枠』を設け、民間投資を促す支援策が検討されている。具体的
な支援策が整えば、複数年度にわたる投資計画の実行を支えよう。 
なお、11月3日に投開票された米国中間選挙の結果を受け、米国におけるデータセンター投資が下振
れるリスクにも触れておきたい。同選挙では、データセンター建設計画の多いテキサス州やジョージ
ア州の州知事選などにおいて、建設の一時停止を掲げる民主党候補が勝利した。米国では、データセ
ンター建設規制に関して州政府や地方自治体が大きな権限を持つ。 
実際、市場ではAI関連企業の業績・投資下振れが意識され、グローバルにハイテク銘柄の株価が急
落した。仮に今後、米国でデータセンター建設が規制されれば、日本でも半導体・電子部品や製造装
置などの輸出が減少し、企業業績が下押しされる可能性がある。その際、AI関連財が減産に転じて設
備不足感が弱まれば、日本国内における能力増強投資の需要も後退しかねない。新知事の任期開始お
よび新議会の招集は2027年1月だが、当面の米国政治動静については注意深くみていく必要があろう。

1 なお、供給制約に起因して、資本財の輸入浸透度（国内供給に占める輸入品の割合）が一層上昇する可能性には留意が必要で
ある。今後、国内の供給余力が限られるなかで投資需要が一層輸入品に向かえば、国産資本財への需要が減退し、投資需要増に
よる GDP の押し上げ効果が限られるリスクがある。

## Document 111: 261108_ms_cotech

### Page 1

M
Update
Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
In-line 3Q26 results
Co-Tech reported 3Q26 results:
• 3Q revenue was NT$3,169mn (+13% q/q, +60% y/y).
• GM was 26.7% (-70bps q/q, +620bps y/y), 20/150bps below MSe/Street,
which we attributed to higher copper pricing and electricity rates (link).
• OP came in at NT$757mn (+11% q/q, +125% y/y), 1% above MSe but 5% below
the Street, for OpM of 23.9% (-50bps q/q, +690bps y/y), 30bps above MSe
but 140bps below the Street.

• As a result, NP was NT$608mn (+12% q/q, +140% y/y), in line with MSe but
4% below the Street, for EPS of NT$2.41.
• Co-Tech’s board also approved up to NT$7bn of CBs to fund the company's
Malaysian expansion. Issue price will be set by auction. Conversion price is
TBD pending regulatory approval.
• Reiterate OW; PT NT$745.
See also our initiation report: Asia Pacific Technology: AI’s Hidden Backbone (13
Sep 2026)

of 9.3% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 8.7% and risk-free rate of 1%), medium-term growth
rate of 11.5%, and terminal growth rate of 3%.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 253: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000 원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000 원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

### Page 7

LG 에너지솔루션

당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
No
vember 2, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

but resilient economic growth, strong

### Page 2

2 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015,1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries,2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks.4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index.5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.

### Page 4

4 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.

not possible to invest directly in an index.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
09:49 November 13 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest
reading since late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of severe economic stress.
Renewed cost-of-living and interest rate pressures were front and centre following another lift in

inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage
holders are feeling the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group falling to a record low.

### Page 2

Consumers have become noticeably more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the relevant
sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge
up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business survey showed confidence slipped further
to –15 in October as conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic. Of concern for the
outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow materially faster than selling prices, further
compressing already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter financial conditions can jolt activity. The
value of new dwelling finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly decline in the series’
history, taking approvals 25% lower year-to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined with the housing tax changes announced in May.

Owner-occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home buyers proving particularly sensitive
to the deterioration in affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum across the domestic economy,
consistent with Westpac’s expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the

consensus view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the result, but core inflation was
also marginally stronger than forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the past four
months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost
inflation through. Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace, gaining 0.3% in
October.
The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor

Waller prior to the release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and the labour market while
showing significant concern over the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the passthrough
of energy and technology input inflation. His conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the
monthly rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward supporting an increase in our policy
rate of 25 basis points at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI inflation data out

later today are similar to the past two monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent or
even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also kept the focus on inflation risks and
stated or implied the FOMC needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s updates,
pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by
September. We believe this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core inflation ex

shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-month annualised basis and the labour market is
balanced; but it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate, US households are under pressure from
the cost of living, and their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing costs. Highlighting

### Page 3

Related articles
this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and 2010. Our November Market Outlook will
provide a full update on our expectations for the US and the world when released on Westpac IQ this
afternoon.

### Page 2

Weekly Economics
U.S. Week Ahead
Existing Home Sales • Thursday
Housing market conditions remain highly unfavorable. Mortgage
rates are the primary headwind, and they have climbed even higher
alongside the rise in longer-term yields. At the same time, low
supply is preventing a broad correction in home prices, leaving
prospective buyers squeezed on two fronts.
We expect that existing home sales slid 3.4% in August to an
annualized rate of 3.92 million. Behind this forecast is ongoing

weakness in mortgage applications and a recent bounce in
mortgage rates, which rose from 6.43% at the beginning of July to
6.66% by month-end.
We do not expect affordability conditions to meaningfully improve
in the near term. Supply is likely to remain constrained while high
mortgage rates discourage mobility. And mortgage rates are apt
to remain elevated amid firm economic growth expectations and
heavy government and corporate debt issuance.

### Page 3

Economics Week Ahead Economics
CPI • Friday
Headline inflation looks to have picked up last month. We estimate
the CPI rose 0.40% in August, with gasoline prices increasing a little
over 4% as renewed tensions in the Middle East pushed oil higher.
Grocery prices also likely rebounded after modestly declining in July.
Outside of food and energy, price pressures appear little changed.
We expect the core CPI to rise 0.23%, essentially matching July's

pace. Core goods inflation should remain firm, led by another solid
increase in used vehicle prices as consumer prices continue to
catch up to the trend indicated at wholesale auctions. Meantime,
household furnishings and other tariff-sensitive categories appear
to be stabilizing after declining earlier this year.
Core services inflation should be little changed with another 0.2%
monthly increase. But underneath the surface, we expect a modest
pickup in travel (led by lodging away from home) to offset slightly

cooler medical care and housing cost growth.
All told, we expect the report to show that while headline inflation
remains influenced by the ebbs and flows of the conflict in the
Middle East, core inflation remains contained. Should our forecast
prove correct, core CPI will ease to 2.4% year-over-year, in line with
its 6-month annualized rate but above the more recent 3-month
annualized pace of 1.7%.

### Page 4

Weekly Economics
G10 Week Ahead
European Central Bank Monetary Policy Meeting •
Thursday
We expect the European Central Bank (ECB) to raise the Deposit
Rate by 25 bps to 2.50% next week. August headline inflation
remained elevated at 3.3% year-over-year, though softer core
and services inflation suggest underlying pressures continue to
moderate. Still, the more hawkish tone of the July minutes and
renewed rise in energy prices should keep policymakers cautious.

The recent escalation in the Middle East has pushed fuel prices
at the pump close to their 2022 peaks, while natural gas storage
remains below historical levels, leaving the Eurozone vulnerable
to further energy price increases. The ECB is unlikely to respond
aggressively if the shock remains confined to energy given the
downside risks to growth. However, a broader rise in food inflation
linked to El Niño would be more concerning, as highly visible prices

could lift inflation expectations and generate second-round effects.
For now, resilient Q3 activity, elevated headline inflation and
contained wage growth support one final hike. The ECB will likely
retain and emphasize its scenario-based outlook in the updated
staff projections given continued uncertainty around the conflict
and energy prices. The policy path beyond September will depend
on whether energy and food pressures persist and begin to spread,

which could leave room for further tightening.

U.K. Monthly GDP • Friday
We expect UK GDP to rise 0.2% month-over-month in July, with
growth of 0.3% on a three-month-over-three-month basis.

Activity likely moderated after June’s strong rebound, though
services remained supportive as the July services PMI rose to 52.1.
Retail sales volumes fell 0.5% month-over-month, while softer
manufacturing activity likely reflected some reversal of earlier
precautionary stockbuilding.
The moderation appears more consistent with payback from
June than a broader deterioration in momentum. August PMIs
remain consistent with Q3 GDP growth of around 0.3% quarter-

over-quarter. Still, the renewed escalation in the Middle East and
associated rise in gas and energy prices adds upside risk to inflation
and could weigh on household demand. Against this backdrop and
with Bank of England hawks continuing to emphasize the need for
tighter policy, we expect the BoE to remain cautious and continue
to look for a rate hike in Q4. -1.0%

### Page 5

Economics Week Ahead Economics
EM Week Ahead
Mexico CPI • Wednesday
We expect Mexico’s August inflation rate to edge higher, with
headline CPI rising to 3.34% year-over-year (0.27% month-over-
month) from 3.26% year-over-year in early August. Part of the
pickup likely reflects less favorable base effects and a smaller
disinflationary contribution from food prices. While agricultural
prices continued to help restrain inflation in mid-August, the drag

was notably less pronounced than in July. Core inflation should
remain elevated at 3.98% year-over-year (0.25% month-over-
month).
While inflation remains within Banxico’s midpoint 3% target,
persistent core and services inflation suggest convergence toward
the midpoint of the target could prove gradual. For now, we
continue to expect Banxico to keep rates on hold, with the policy
rate remaining at 6.50% through the rest of 2026 and into early
2027 as policymakers await clearer evidence that underlying

inflation pressures are easing sustainably. While rate cuts remain
a possibility in 2027, the current inflation backdrop argues for
patience in the near term.

Brazil CPI • Friday
We expect Brazil’s August inflation rate to slow to 4.20% year-over-
year, helped by lower electricity, food and fuel prices. The main
driver should be a temporary reduction in residential electricity bills
stemming from a one-off credit, while recent data already point to
a notable easing in price pressures. Mid-August CPI came in weaker
than expected, with inflation falling to 4.24% year-over-year amid
declines in electricity, food, fuel and airfare prices. Beyond these

temporary factors, restrictive monetary policy continues to weigh
on economic activity, as evidenced by the recent slowdown in Q2
GDP growth. However, underlying inflation risks remain. A still-
tight labor market and government measures aimed at supporting
consumption could keep services inflation sticky, making progress
toward the 3.0% inflation target more gradual.
Overall, the inflation outlook remains consistent with additional
policy easing. We expect COPOM to deliver a 25 bps rate cut in

September, bringing the Selic Rate to 13.75%. Beyond September,
policymakers are likely to proceed cautiously as they assess
incoming inflation data and the impact of the election cycle on
inflation expectations. Our base case remains for a pause after
September, with additional easing more likely in 2027.

## Document 336: First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ labour market surveys, September quarter 2026 (2026-11-14).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-14
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ labour market surveys, September quarter
2026
By Michael Gordon
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:01 November 04 2026 Share
The unemployment rate rose to 5.7% in the September quarter, against expectations for a
small decline. Spare capacity in the labour market is keeping wage pressures subdued.
Unemployment rate: 5.7% (prev: 5.6%, Westpac: 5.5%, RBNZ: 5.5%, mkt: 5.5%)
Employment change: -0.1% (prev: +0.3%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.3%, mkt: +0.4%)

### Page 2

Participation rate: 70.4% (prev: 70.6%, Westpac: 70.8%, RBNZ: 70.6%, mkt: 70.7%) 
Labour costs (private sector): +0.5% (prev: +0.7%, Westpac: +0.5%, RBNZ: +0.4%, mkt: +0.5%)

The September quarter surveys pointed to ongoing softness in New Zealand’s labour market. The
unemployment rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing an almost five-year run of increases.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-
living pressures. 
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise 0.1% fall in employment in the
September quarter, after a downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That went against
the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward
momentum emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure had outpaced the MEI for the
previous nine quarters). It was also softer than suggested by the business-focused Quarterly
Employment Survey (QES), which recorded a 0.2% rise in full-time equivalent employees, a 0.1% rise
in filled jobs and a 0.6% rise in hours paid. 
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in labour force participation rate, from 70.6%
to 70.4%. These co-movements are effectively a reversal of the surprisingly large increases that we
saw in the June quarter, which we noted at the time were indicative of sampling error in the survey. 
This is why we generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate, as it’s less vulnerable to
sampling error. And in that respect, the message on the jobs market remains weak: to the extent that
employment may be rising, it’s still not fast enough to absorb the growth in the population (which is
itself a fairly low bar, as net migration remains at a relatively low ebb). 
The HLFS also recorded a sharp 1.6% fall in hours worked (although usual hours worked were down
just 0.4%). This seems to have been influenced by Matariki – a floating holiday that was only
introduced a few years ago, so will not be picked up by the seasonal adjustment. This may have also
been responsible for a marked shift from full-time to part-time employment, as some people may have
been pushed into the part-time category simply by virtue of only working four days during the survey
week. 
The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September quarter, in line with our expectations
overall but a bit weaker in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while there was a
surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector wages. There was a notable jump in the number of
employers citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay rates, although the size of
those increases varied a great deal, with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate. 
Overall, we think the details are soft from the RBNZ’s perspective – an important consideration ahead
of the 9 December Monetary Policy Statement, as there are few other major data releases between
now and then. The unemployment rate is a more reliable signal than the employment measures, and
the ongoing rise in unemployment points to more slack in the labour market than the RBNZ was hoping
to see in an economy that has been growing for over a year now. The doves on the policy committee
will be encouraged in their view that there is limited scope for headline inflation shocks to pass through
into second-round effects. 
Michael Gordon, Senior Economist
P: +64 21 749 506

## Document 341: FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key calls

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key calls.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

We expect 2027 to be a low-volatility year in FX, with central
banks and rate diﬀerentials ﬁrmly in the driving seat. We see a
dollar decline resuming from 2Q27, while diverging monetary,
growth and external dynamics create opportunities across
regions

Chris Turner, Francesco Pesole, Frantisek Taborsky and Deepali
Bhargava
Winners and losers in FX next year

### Page 2

Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Not a breakout year for volatility
Our most important FX call for 2027 is the market environment. Our
macro-driven call is that lower oil prices and only a modest
recalibration in global monetary policy mean that FX volatility can
stay low. This is not 2022/2023, when major central banks
belatedly delivered 500bp tightening cycles to deal with double-
digit inﬂation. If 2026 has shown us anything, it is that FX markets
can prove quite resilient in this high-growth, high-inﬂation global

economy. And markets have proved quite resilient to geopolitical
risk as well.
For the majority of the last ﬁve years, the market has
overestimated FX volatility. There may be some structural factors at
play here, such as huge growth in ﬁnancial assets bringing in more
liquidity to the FX market and the greater use of FX pricing algos on
the buy-side, limiting the market fallout when trading conditions
thin.
Yet market volatility has not vanished. There are plenty of risks out

there, which we highlight in a dedicated section below. The same
applies for market liquidity. If the shock is big enough – such as
Liberation Day tariﬀs in 2025 or the start of the Iran war this year –
market depth can fall away quickly. The prospect of fat tails, low-
probability, high-impact event risks, is certainly a threat to global
FX markets. But the bar for these risks to raise volatility is a high
one.

### Page 3

Volatility has mostly undershot expectations
in 2026
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Look no further than central banks in
G10
In a year dominated by geopolitical risk and energy shock, currency
investors quickly moved beyond headline trading and increasingly
looked to short-term rate diﬀerentials as the primary guide for
currencies. We expect that to remain the case next year.
Central bank tightening is moving beyond its “insurance” phase,
requiring evidence that inﬂationary pressures are becoming

persistent. Oil prices will remain part of the story, but we think the
more important distinction is between economies where the shock
can generate more lasting inﬂation and those where it cannot. On
top of that, the central banks’ FX-speciﬁc approach will be key.
Remember how, in 2022-2024, the fear of ampliﬁed inﬂation
eﬀects from a weak currency prompted tighter ﬁscal policies
among some often dovish-leaning central banks.
Here are our top picks among G10 currencies for next year:

1. JPY – the best risk/reward. We don’t have a huge
outperformance of the yen in our forecasts, especially relative
to the euro. But that’s primarily due to our assumption that the
euro will drop its French-related risk premium by end-2028, and

### Page 4

a carry-favourable environment can somewhat contain yen
gains. However, we think the yen oﬀers the best risk-reward
proﬁle in G10. Alongside being sharply undervalued based on
long-term fundamentals, it faces limited risks of dovish
repricing as the Bank of Japan’s tightening cycle predated the
Iran war. It incidentally has strong safe-haven value against
some fat-tail risks (AI bust, ﬁscal dominance, dollar
debasement, some geopolitical events) and stands to be a

beneﬁciary of an ultimate capitulation in USD rates. Downside
risks are, crucially, limited by a coordinated Tokyo-Washington
eﬀort to prevent sharp USD/JPY rallies.
2. CHF – SNB turning pro-franc. If 2027 is the year when global
interest rates peak and then reverse, the opportunity cost of
holding the franc starts to fall. We also think the Swiss National
Bank will prove more tolerant of franc strength. Not only are
there fewer complaints about the strong franc from local

corporates, but Switzerland’s inﬂation is much lower than
trading partners and the SNB might again opt for nominal
franc appreciation to keep the real exchange rate stable.
3. NZD – The high-beta gamble. Among higher-beta currencies,
we see good potential for the New Zealand dollar next year. The
Reserve Bank of New Zealand has delivered a few dovish
surprises, but we think it is underestimating the inﬂation risks
for 2027: we expect at least two hikes, also helped by a fairly

robust growth backdrop. NZD should be favoured against the
Australian dollar to play a decline in global rates, as the
Reserve Bank of Australia may already shift to cuts in the third
quarter of 2027.
Sterling, dollar and AUD face largest dovish
repricing risk

### Page 5

Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Dollar decline is delayed, not cancelled
Our baseline for global inﬂation in 2027 remains benign, leaving
most of our major central bank calls more dovish than current
market pricing. We expect the Federal Reserve to deliver one ﬁnal
hike in December 2026, taking rates to a 4.25% peak, but we don’t
think the additional hikes priced by 3Q27 will materialise. Modest
second-round eﬀects should fail to sustain inﬂation once the 2Q27

base-eﬀect decline comes through. Importantly, and unlike in other
economies, we think benign inﬂation rather than weaker growth
will be the main catalyst for a dovish Fed turn. That means markets
may be reluctant to price a large Fed cutting cycle too early, but it’s
equally an environment where we envision a Fed cut in 4Q27.
Ultimately, we expect a nearly 100bp drop in the 2-year USD swap
rate by the end of 2027, with most of the move concentrated in the

year’s second half.
We expect the dollar to weaken alongside that repricing, with
greater conviction in the downside story later in the year. The Fed
should remain cautious about turning dovish before clearer
evidence of disinﬂation emerges, while elevated funding rates will
continue to make it expensive to sell or hedge dollars until some
easing is priced in. And because we also hold dovish views on other
central banks, we don’t expect narrower USD short-term rate

diﬀerentials to trigger a particularly large dollar downturn. We
target a roughly 5% decline in DXY to 97 by end-2027.

Front-end rates to drive DXY lower
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Bond premium on the euro needs bad
news fuel
Our colleagues in the macro and rates team remain cautious about
a recovery in French sentiment in the next couple of quarters. Weak
growth (0.5% in 2027) should continue to make ﬁscal arithmetic
complicated, and we forecast a deﬁcit of 5.5% next year –
assuming a budget is passed – above the 5.0% government target.
Election frontrunner Marine Le Pen has signalled large spending

cuts, although their credibility remains in question. She is, anyway,
a more market-friendly candidate than left-wing rival Jean-Luc
Mélenchon.
We think markets will be wary of jumping into a meaningful OAT
relief rally before the presidential election (18 April-2 May) and likely
parliamentary elections in the summer are out of the way. We are
not forecasting another big selloﬀ in French bonds but think 125-
150bp will be the norm for the OAT-Bund 10yr spread into 2Q27,

before some tightening towards 100bp in 2H27.
The euro has been embedding a French risk premium since early
October, meaning a further material deterioration in sentiment
would probably be needed to generate additional idiosyncratic
pressure. Historically, the link between euro-area sovereign spreads

Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Central Europe: Same shocks, different
paths for local FX
The Central Europe region is entering a year of divergence. The
Czech Republic, Poland and Hungary face similar risks from energy
prices, geopolitics and volatile global markets, but increasingly
diﬀerent monetary, ﬁscal and growth dynamics mean CEE3 is
becoming less of a single FX trade.

In the Czech Republic, the Czech National Bank has already
delivered two hikes, taking rates to 4%, and remains the most
hawkish central bank in the region. If inﬂation proves more
persistent, further tightening remains possible. Positive nominal
and real rate diﬀerentials and high credibility should therefore keep
the koruna relatively defensive during periods of global uncertainty,

### Page 8

while any easing of external risks could put it back on an
appreciation path.
Poland is moving diﬀerently. The National Bank of Poland is still
waiting to deliver its ﬁrst hike, leaving the zloty with less monetary
support for now. Strong growth and sizeable EU inﬂows remain
important anchors, but narrower rate diﬀerentials, loose ﬁscal
policy and rising political and rating risks have weakened PLN’s
relative appeal. We therefore see the zloty as more vulnerable to

higher global yields, energy prices and geopolitical risks.
Hungary is heading in the opposite direction, with the National
Bank of Hungary having cut rates in recent months. We expect
another cut only later next year, provided global conditions allow it.
Attractive carry, EU-fund inﬂows and an improving external balance
should support the forint, while progress towards euro adoption
provides another positive longer-term story. The central bank’s

focus on the exchange rate should meanwhile limit the pace of
easing and keep EUR/HUF biased towards the stronger end of its
range.

Asia: Rotation underway, tech currencies

### Page 9

A softer dollar should provide a broad tailwind for Asian FX,
particularly in the second half of 2027, but we expect performance
to remain highly diﬀerentiated. We favour the Korean won and
Taiwanese dollar, expect further renminbi appreciation – albeit with
greater two-way volatility – and see continued downside risks for
the Indonesian rupiah and Philippine peso. We are more
constructive on the Indian rupee.
Current account strength should continue to favour the tech

currencies. Although the AI cycle may be plateauing, activity
remains high and strong export revenues should support the KRW
and TWD. Exporter FX conversion and measures encouraging the
repatriation of overseas assets should provide additional support.
CNY should also beneﬁt from strong external balances, although
we expect a less one-directional appreciation trend.
Rate support will become less favourable. Most Asian central banks
are further advanced in their tightening cycles than the Fed and

should ﬁnish hiking by early 2027 before preparing for easing.
Narrowing rate support should particularly weigh on the rate-
sensitive IDR. Continued household and corporate demand for
foreign assets could also oﬀset some of the beneﬁts from strong
current accounts.
Lower oil and food prices should help importers in the second half
of 2027, but capital ﬂows are unlikely to recover evenly. Political
uncertainty and the MSCI downgrade should also remain

headwinds for the IDR, while weaker FDI inﬂows leave the PHP
vulnerable. By contrast, we favour the INR, where resilient growth,
supply-chain diversiﬁcation and continued FDI should provide
support, while leaving India less exposed to swings in the AI cycle.

### Page 10

Source: ING, Macrobond
Latam: Banxico’s neglect of the peso
stands out
We’ve said that central bank policy is going to be more important
than ever in 2027. And exchange rates can be an important part of
the monetary toolkit – especially in small, open economies. In a
world where central bank tightening cycles are being priced more
hawkishly, Banxico’s unabashedly dovish stance stands out.
When cutting rates to 6.25% in November, Banxico noted that,

because of slack economic conditions, the pass-through of
exchange rate ﬂuctuations to consumer prices would be limited.
The IMF puts that economic ‘slack’ – or the negative output gap – at
0.4% of GDP in 2027. That makes Banxico far less concerned about
a weaker currency than most other central banks.
In addition, Banxico has now cut the peso’s policy rate protection to
just 225bp over the Fed. That is a record low and leaves the peso
vulnerable to any deterioration in the external environment. We see

the peso as a relative underperformer in 2027.
Further south, the Brazilian real has been causing a lot of pain to
investors recently. The fear is that President Luiz Inácio Lula da
Silva’s pre-election giveaways will come home to roost next year.
His choice of Finance Minister is going to be important, as will any
progress on a 2027 budget. Preventing a bigger sell-oﬀ in the real,
however, is the independent central bank running very high real

GIS-KY(6456 TT)       法說/座談摘要 
雷射封裝業務進展順利，2Q27有望顯著放量 
⚫ 公司積極轉型自傳統觸控模組轉向半導體光學 
⚫ 雷射封測試量產進行中，預期年底出貨，2027將有顯著貢獻 
結論：GIS-KY 3Q26營收有起色，但仍受終端市場需求不振及客戶端記
憶體缺貨的影響，但整體財務結構與淨現金部位仍穩健。公司目前正積
極推動業務轉型，從傳統觸控模組轉向半導體光學領域，並已擴大資本
支出佈局光通訊雷射光源封測與 AR/VR等新產品，預計這些新事業將於
2027至 2028年成為公司顯著的營收成長動能。

3Q26財務數據 
⚫ 營收 144億元(QoQ+37.6%，YoY-10.5%)，主要受到終端市場需求
不振及客戶端記憶體缺貨的影響。 
⚫ 毛利率 4.02%(QoQ+0.68ppts，YoY-2.46ppts) 
⚫ 營業費用 12.11億元，前三季單季皆維持在 11.2億元左右的水準。
因公司處於轉型期，於 R&D的投資相對較多。 
⚫ 營業利益-6.3億元 
⚫ 稅前淨利-6.69億元 
⚫ 單季 EPS -3.15元 
⚫ 稅後淨利-7.25億元 
⚫ 單季 EPS -2.17元。 
產品營收占比 
⚫ 3Q26：平板佔 40%、筆電佔 28%、指紋辨識模組佔 13%、其他佔
19%。 
⚫ 1Q-3Q26：平板佔 32%、筆電佔 21%、指紋辨識模組佔 9%、其他
佔 38%。 
法說會內容摘要 
1.  公司中長期轉型策略：半導體光學(Semi Optics) 
• 光學元件：主要應用於 AR/VR眼鏡、Waveguide及抬頭顯示器
（HUD），涵蓋奈米壓印、蝕刻及鍍膜等製程，目前擁有多個國際大廠
專案。預期 AR/VR軟硬體需至 2027至 2028年才會成熟發酵。 
• 雷射封裝：為近期資本支出重點方向，主力產品為 CW Laser CoC
以及未來的 VCSEL VOD。

Q&A內容摘要 
1. Q：新世代半導體光學產品的資本支出目前的廠區規劃及進度為何？後續大客戶的營
收比重是否會下降？  
A：公告約 80億元的資本支出中，雷射封裝相關預計佔 40至 50億元，約三分之一投入台
灣廠區，其餘投入海外越南新廠。剩下的資本支出將用於 Micro LED、AR/VR波導及大客
戶新產品（廠區分散於大陸及台灣）。與大陸大客戶合作近 20年互信基礎深厚，只要有觸
控與顯示的新產品皆會參與，大客戶仍是重要客戶，但隨新業務逐步發酵，其營收佔比的
確會漸漸下降。

2. Q：10月份營收跳升破百億元的原因？這是一次性的嗎？這是否會佔用過多營運資
金？  
A：10月營收達 130億元，主因為客戶委託進行手機關鍵零組件的加工與精密對位貼合，
這是相對長期的專案，並非一次性。營收跳升主力來自高單價材料，若純看加工費，實際
營收佔比並不高。材料確實會佔用較大營運資金，但因週期短，銀行額度支持無虞；先前
提出的增資案主要是為支撐長期營運資金及較大資本支出。

3. Q：雷射封裝產線何時建好並貢獻營收？GIS在此領域相對於現有供應商的優勢在
哪？  
A：產線今年 2Q26已陸續建置，4Q26客戶原材料供應及認證趨於成熟，量產預計在 11月
或 12月從小量往上衝。GIS的優勢在於：(1)長期與國際大廠合作累積的製造管理能力。(2)
擁有充足現金流與財務實力，能支撐數十億的資本支出。

4. Q：雷射出貨的規格？今明兩年的資本支出預估？  
A：目前專注於大於 300mW、400mW的高功率雷射（不涉獵傳統 100mW），主要應用於
ELSFP等 200G以上高階領域，材料主力為磷化銦，同時也開發 CW Laser及 VCSEL封裝。
今年已核准資本支出約 80億元，明年資本支出將視新產品狀況及客戶需求而定，預計今年
底或明年初會較明確。

5. Q：明年筆電與平板的記憶體缺貨狀況會好轉嗎？  
A：據業界交流，因新產能開出尚需時間，記憶體缺貨狀況到 2027年底前改善幅度可能有
限，許多人預估缺貨可能持續到 2028年。

6. Q：FAU的佈局？與集團內的訊芯有無合作？  
A：集團內公司皆獨立營運，訊芯比較接近光模塊加工。GIS初期不會聚焦整套 FAU模組
組裝，而是先從 passive component（如 Fiber holder、V型槽、透鏡陣列、超透鏡）做起，
未來若元件做得不錯，才會考慮後段的模組。

7. Q：大客戶即將推出搭載 OLED及觸控功能的筆電，貴司是否有參與？ A：不便針對
單一客戶產品發表評論。但只要是分離式的觸控模組（非內嵌式 In-cell Touch），公司都會
積極爭取並參與。

8. Q：雷射封裝做到 300mW以上，在技術或設備上有何門檻與優勢？ A：100mW提升
到 300mW或 400mW，封裝的製程流程基本相同，但測試項目及設備必須大幅更新，這也
是新資本支出的重點。基於過去在顯示器背光模組及指紋辨識貼合所累積的光學製程經
驗，跨入此領域對公司來說並非太難。

### Page 4

【中信投顧投資分析報告】 
個股摘要

2026 /11/10

4 
9. Q：設備交期似乎很長，何時能滿產？ 
A：固晶機、打線機是泛用的，依照現有機台去挪動不難，主要是測試設備（如測試設備
外掛的電流計、頻譜分析儀）交期確實較長，預計 2Q27季底到 3Q27初產能可完全開出。
2Q27量產時預計會有一到兩家客戶。

10. Q：滿產能時的營收貢獻預估？何時能回收資本支出？  
A：雷射封裝商業模式以加工費為主，營收絕對金額佔比看似較小，但扣除材料費後，純
加工毛利率遠高於傳統觸控顯示業務。董事會對資本支出有嚴格的財務紀律，原則上必須
在少數幾年內完成回收。

11. Q：到 2H27可能 300mW以上的雷射市場變成熟，公司屆時產能到位可以拿下多少市
佔? 
A：公司有接觸多位 high power的客戶，每個客戶需求都很可觀，即便現在因為選舉政治
議題等潛在變數可能改變 AI的需求，但以整個產業來看，公司建置的產能僅占需求面的
一部分比例，仍有很大的增加產能的空間。

12. Q：與富采的 Micro LED及光通訊發展有何競爭或關聯？  
A：富采沒有做到封裝，GIS做封裝，雙方可能為上下游關係。富采 Micro LED偏向 TV巨
量轉移應用，GIS則聚焦 AR投影機光機應用，兩者 Micro LED大小差異很大。公司也與
客戶在探討 Micro LED或 Micro VCSEL在光通訊領域的潛力，均處於樣品開發階段。另外
公司今年 4Q26或 1Q27 AR projector可能會有小量出貨。

13. Q：光通訊相關的研發專案進度？以及信賴性測試（如 5000小時）的狀況？FAU會朝
模組發展嗎？  
A：不便針對單一客戶及具體的信賴性測試時數評論。關於 FAU，初期聚焦於光學元件（如
Fiber holder、波導、V型槽、超透鏡）。公司已具備 Metalens自行設計及製造的能力，因
其製程與目前開發的 AR眼鏡波導製程十分相近。

資本支出將較過去三年進一步顯著提高。

► 美國9 月CPI 、PPI 皆上升， 且 超 過 市 場 預
期，9月PPI月增率為1.3%，創2022年來最

達6.3%，為2022年12月來最大漲幅，顯示

固性使市場預期高利率將維持更久時間，美

利、股債皆挫，顯示目前即使是AI、科技信

資產同步下挫。

► 美國9月CPI年增率上升至3.7%，核心CPI年
增率由2.4%升至2.6%，核心月增率0.3%，
高於預期值0.2%。能源是通膨反彈的主因，
貢獻整體CPI月增幅超過一半；能源衝擊雖
為主導，但消費韌性使企業保有成本轉嫁能
力，住房與部分服務價格維持黏性，需求面
通膨略有提高，加上企業資本支出提升經濟
與企業獲利韌性，使總需求不易快速降溫。
► 凱投預期Fed 12月將升息1碼，明年3月再升
1碼，政策利率可能在4%以上維持更久。受
到油價高盤、通膨僵固，與財政惡化、政府
發債籌資逢企業債搶錢等影響，美歐日主要
國家公 債利 率近 期 來到高 位 。其中， 法國
2027年財政預算欲刪減支出(包括養老金、公
部門工資等)引發民眾反彈，衝擊主權信用評
等，法國CDS與法德10年期公債利差擴大、
歐元走弱，美元轉強，商品、黃金承壓。法
國政治不確定性讓歐股修正、法股最疲，成
熟國家公債殖利率升高，增加企業借貸成本
與股市評價壓力，續為金融市場擾動因子。

► 美國第三季財報季展開，市場將觀察企業在
高利率環境下的獲利表現。根據FactSet數據
顯示，標普500指數第三季獲利成長預估達
29.5%，顯示企業基本面仍具支撐，其中科
技 產業 受 惠AI需求帶動， 獲 利 成 長預 估達
65%，持續扮演企業獲利的重要成長動能。
► 聚焦科技類股，AI資本支出仍為本輪企業獲
利成長的主要驅動力。高盛指出，第三季AI

基礎建設相關企業將貢獻標普500獲利增長
的54%。此外，大型CSP之雲端業務營收有
望加速成長，反映AI運算及雲端服務需求持
續提升，有助支撐後續資本支出擴張動能。
► 近期市場波動仍受高利率環境影響，壓抑投
資人風險偏好與估值擴張空間，顯示市場目
前壓力更多來自評價面而非基本面。隨財報
數據陸續公布，若企業持續繳出優於預期的
獲利表現，並維持正向財測，市場有望逐步
回歸基本面評價，使企業獲利成長逐步支撐
股市表現。
美企財報獲利穩健，對股市中長期有撐
熱門議題
美股第三季獲利成長持續上修，科技為主要支撐 CSP雲端營收加速，資本支出變現跡象持續受驗證
評價面疑慮下，股價難以進一步擴張
熱門議題財報展望

投資焦點
► 醫療保健、醫療服務與生物技術等行業，在
景氣不好時，市場預期民眾對醫療保健產業
的需求 不易 下降 ， 成為傳 統防禦 型類 股代
表。目前金融環境利率偏高，11月美國總統
大選國會恐變天，股市波動風險上升，觀察
過去當VIX恐慌指數上揚時，醫療保健產業
相較於科技股更穩健，具有更好抗震能力。
► 醫療與生技類股近一年漲幅明顯，主因1)生
技股過去一段時間受到高利率、融資成本及
臨床試驗風險壓抑，部分公司估值偏低。當
臨床數據改善、或導入AI篩選資料與藥物，
投資人重新評價未來現金流與價值。2)大型
藥廠面臨既有藥品專利到期壓力，透過收購
取得新藥與研發管線，提高有潛力的生技公
司併購價值，帶動整體產業估值回升。3)肥
胖、癌症與慢性病用藥創造長期市場需求，
創新藥物(如瘦瘦針)讓部分醫療公司成長不
再只依賴人口老化，高盛今年7月將全球減肥
藥市場2030年銷售額預估上調15%至1,140
億美元，看好此類藥品的長期商業潛力。
醫療生技近年表現佳，估今年3Q醫院客流量年增4%VIX恐慌指數上升時，健護醫療表現相較穩健

內部參考文件請勿外流
► 美國醫療費用深受法令影響，由於商業雇主
贊助保險(ESI)和平價醫療法案(ACA)的自付
額增加，以及最高自付費用要求，較去年同
期提高，使今年第三季美國整體住院人數、
手術量和醫院客流量均呈現改善。高盛預估
今年第三季醫院客流量年增4%，加上季節性
因素，下半年醫療保健利用率將較上半年成
長，整體第四季預估獲利穩健，甚至可延至
明年，反應在近期XLV ETF股價創新高。
► 展望2027年，各醫療保險計畫普遍優先考慮
透過提高會員保費，和要求費用分攤(尤其是
提高會員自付費用上限)來擴大利潤空間，並
持續優化覆蓋範圍和產品組合，預估商業雇
主提供的醫療保險(ESI)保費趨勢，將在明年
加速成長至過去20 年來的最高水準。 以
Refinitiv預估明年美國各產業獲利成長來看，
醫療保健和生技相較於今年獲利明顯增加，
反而今年獲利極佳的能源和通訊受高基期影
響，獲利增速下滑。投資人在整體金融環境
波動放大時期，宜以醫療生技股穩中求利。
投資焦點
近3個月漲幅和預估獲利呈正成長，醫療生技股佳

股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長，市場靜待財報結果與展望，和對AI資本支出回收影響。
考量地緣衝突、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉，短期金融市場紛擾不止，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變，逢股市拉回時可分批布局，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點，全球利率走揚有利銀行股。此外，地緣衝突頻傳，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間，殖利率偏高環境使短中期債券提供吸
引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利
差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等

匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。

人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。

### Page 17

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。
